# Tenant isolation — Post trio A/B measurement

> Generated by `scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts`. The THREE generated sections below — the
> before capture, the after capture, and the policy form decision run — are written
> between markers, each independently; everything outside them, including every reading
> and the completion report, is hand-written and survives a re-run.

## Method, and what it does not claim

Plans are captured through a session whose `current_user` is `omnipost_app` — the
non-superuser, non-bypassing role the application connects as — with `app.account_id`
bound exactly as `PrismaUnitOfWork` binds it. A plan taken as the owner would carry no
policy qual and would understate every query here.

Each case pairs a REAL Prisma call from a named repository site with a hand-written SQL
mirror, runs both inside the same bound transaction, and compares the returned row
identities before taking any plan. The mirror is what `EXPLAIN` sees, because Prisma 7
emits its SQL through a driver adapter this script cannot subscribe to; the fidelity check
is what stops that mirror from drifting into a different question. Honest limits, stated
rather than implied:

- The mirror is **semantically** validated, not textually identical to Prisma's emission.
  Prisma may render a relation predicate as a join where the mirror writes `EXISTS`;
  where that distinction matters it is called out in the case's notes.
- **The check validates predicate and ordering, NOT projection.** `Q1`, `Q3`, `Q5` and `Q6`
  ask Prisma for `select: { id: true }` while their mirrors select the full column list, so
  the two sides return the same ROWS through different column sets. A mirror that drifted
  only in its projection would pass. That is deliberate — the identity of the rows is what
  decides whether the plan answers the same question — but it is a real limit, so a case
  whose column list matters must not rely on this check to notice.
- **An identity comparison over an empty result cannot fail**, so equal digests are only
  evidence when both sides actually matched something. Every case reports that, and a case
  matching nothing FAILS the run unless it declares itself a miss probe; `Q8` is the one
  such declaration here, and it is checked in reverse too — if it starts matching rows the
  run fails, because its plan would no longer be the empty-bucket plan it claims to be.
  Both guards run BEFORE the report is written, so a capture that failed one leaves nothing
  on disk.
- **The mirror tracks its source site as it is TODAY, not as it was when an earlier phase was
  captured.** Four sites changed emission between the two captures (see §Reading the after
  capture), and the fidelity check cannot detect that class on its own: both texts return the
  same rows, so the comparison passes while the plan describes a query nobody issues. Those
  four are named per case; the other nine are textually identical across both phases.
- Literal values are inlined, so these are **custom** plans. A generic plan for the same
  statement can differ.
- One database, one corpus, one machine. Numbers here are comparable to the other phase of
  the same file and to nothing else.

## Before — pre-migration capture

Captured 2026-09-08T04:48:46.572Z in 0.1 s. PostgreSQL: PostgreSQL 16.14 (Debian 16.14-1.pgdg12+1). Measuring role: `omnipost_app` (non-superuser, non-bypassing), `app.account_id` bound to `tif-ab-a` for every statement below.

**Re-run this exact capture:**

```bash
pnpm db:up
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --phase before --projects 100 --posts 10000 --runs 3
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --cleanup
# The generator emits unpadded Markdown tables and Prettier aligns them, so the
# capture ALWAYS leaves this file formatting-dirty. Run this or the repo gate fails:
pnpm exec prettier --write docs/reports/TENANT_RLS_AB_MEASUREMENT.md
```

### Data shape

Two tenants (`tif-ab-a`, `tif-ab-b`), 100 projects and 10000 posts each, posts round-robined over the tenant's projects; every 20th post soft-deleted, every 10th archived, statuses cycling DRAFT/SCHEDULED/PUBLISHED/FAILED, one `PostContent` per post and one `PostMedia` per third post. `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` ran on all five tables before the capture, so the visibility map is set and index-only-scan costing reflects a settled heap. That is ALL it buys, and the previous wording claimed more: it said the plan was "reproducible across reseeds", which this artifact's own data disproves — `Q4` selects a different index between runs of ONE capture, on a 0.11 % planner tie that nothing here repairs. Where that happens the case carries a per-run index annotation.

| Namespaced rows                               | Count |
| --------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| `Account` (`tif-ab-%`)                        | 2     |
| `Project` (`tif-ab-%`)                        | 200   |
| `Post` (`tif-ab-%`)                           | 20000 |
| `PostContent` (`tif-ab-%`)                    | 20000 |
| `PostMedia` (`tif-ab-%`)                      | 6666  |
| _control: all `Account` rows in the database_ | 356   |

| Table         | Live rows | Planner estimate | Total size | RLS   | FORCE | Owner      | Policies |
| ------------- | --------- | ---------------- | ---------- | ----- | ----- | ---------- | -------- |
| `Project`     | 517       | 517              | 30 MB      | true  | false | `postgres` | 1        |
| `Post`        | 20003     | 20003            | 16 MB      | false | false | `postgres` | 0        |
| `PostContent` | 20003     | 20003            | 16 MB      | false | false | `postgres` | 0        |
| `PostMedia`   | 6666      | 6666             | 2808 kB    | false | false | `postgres` | 0        |

### Hot `Post` listing paths

#### Q1 — listByProject — page 1 of a project's live posts

- **Source site**: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaPostQueryRepository.ts:136 (where built at :185)`
- **Why it is here**: the customer's post list; the most frequently served Post read
- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the Prisma call and `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the mirror.
- **Prisma call wall time**: 25.89 ms
- **Planning time (ms, per run)**: 0.096 / 0.089 / 0.099
- **Execution time (ms, per run)**: 0.040 / 0.034 / 0.036
- **Plan nodes**: Limit → Nested Loop → Index Scan → Materialize → Index Scan
- **Indexes used**: Post_projectId_createdAt_idx, Project_pkey

Query text (the mirror; `EXPLAIN` is taken from exactly this):

```sql
SELECT p.id, p."projectId", p.status, p."scheduledAt", p."publishedAt", p."createdAt", p."updatedAt" FROM "Post" p
 WHERE p."projectId" = 'tif-ab-a-proj-0001'
   AND p."deletedAt" IS NULL
   AND p."archivedAt" IS NULL
   AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Project" pr
                WHERE pr.id = p."projectId" AND pr."accountId" = 'tif-ab-a')
 ORDER BY p."createdAt" DESC
 LIMIT 20 OFFSET 0
```

#### Q2 — listByProject — the paired total count

- **Source site**: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaPostQueryRepository.ts:161`
- **Why it is here**: runs on every page load in the same Promise.all as Q1, over the whole project
- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `100` from the Prisma call and `100` from the mirror.
- **Prisma call wall time**: 3.35 ms
- **Planning time (ms, per run)**: 0.087 / 0.083 / 0.081
- **Execution time (ms, per run)**: 0.037 / 0.035 / 0.035
- **Plan nodes**: Aggregate → Nested Loop → Index Scan → Index Only Scan
- **Indexes used**: Project_pkey, Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx

Query text (the mirror; `EXPLAIN` is taken from exactly this):

```sql
SELECT count(*) AS n FROM "Post" p
 WHERE p."projectId" = 'tif-ab-a-proj-0001'
   AND p."deletedAt" IS NULL
   AND p."archivedAt" IS NULL
   AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Project" pr
                WHERE pr.id = p."projectId" AND pr."accountId" = 'tif-ab-a')
```

#### Q3 — listGlobal — the account-wide feed, page 1

- **Source site**: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaPostQueryRepository.ts:405`
- **Why it is here**: the tenant-wide listing the new (accountId, projectId) partial index exists to serve
- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000002,tif-ab-a-post-000003,tif-ab-a-post-000004,tif-ab-a-post-000005,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the Prisma call and `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000002,tif-ab-a-post-000003,tif-ab-a-post-000004,tif-ab-a-post-000005,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the mirror.

Query text (the mirror; `EXPLAIN` is taken from exactly this):

```sql
SELECT p.id, p."projectId", p.status, p."scheduledAt", p."publishedAt", p."createdAt", p."updatedAt" FROM "Post" p
 WHERE p."deletedAt" IS NULL
   AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Project" pr
                WHERE pr.id = p."projectId"
                  AND pr."accountId" = 'tif-ab-a'
                  AND pr."deletedAt" IS NULL)
 ORDER BY p."createdAt" DESC
 LIMIT 20 OFFSET 0
```

#### Q4 — listGlobal — the paired total count

- **Source site**: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaPostQueryRepository.ts:436`
- **Why it is here**: the account-wide count; today it can only be answered by walking into Project
- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `9500` from the Prisma call and `9500` from the mirror.
- **Prisma call wall time**: 2.14 ms

- **Indexes used**: Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx

Query text (the mirror; `EXPLAIN` is taken from exactly this):

```sql
SELECT count(*) AS n FROM "Post" p
 WHERE p."deletedAt" IS NULL
   AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "Project" pr
                WHERE pr.id = p."projectId"
                  AND pr."accountId" = 'tif-ab-a'
                  AND pr."deletedAt" IS NULL)
```

- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the Prisma call and `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the mirror.

### `postId`-led child reads — `PostContent` and `PostMedia`

These two tables are the ones the leg-1 index exemption governs: the design gives them no accountId-led index, on the argument that their reads are parent-key-led and equality is LEAKPROOF, so the caller's filter still index-scans ahead of the policy qual. The plans below are the evidence that argument gets compared against once the columns and policies land.

## Reading the before capture

Hand-written, outside the generated block, so a re-run of `--phase before` does not
overwrite it. Every number below is from the block above.

### The account-wide feed is the one query with no index to answer it

`Q3` (`listGlobal` page 1) is the **only** capture that reaches a sequential scan on `Post`:
4.305 ms, hash-joining the whole table against the tenant's projects, then a top-N sort.
(Every timing quoted in this section is the MEDIAN of the capture's three runs.) Every other
`Post` path index-scans, because every existing `Post` index is `projectId`-led and those
queries supply a `projectId`. The account-wide feed supplies none — the tenant identity
lives one table away — so there is nothing for the planner to use.

`Q3` is the exact query D-S1-1's single new index
(`@@index([accountId, projectId], where: { deletedAt: null })`) exists to serve, and these
are the numbers it will be measured against. Two honest caveats attached now rather than
later: 20 000 posts is a small table, so 4 ms is the shape of the problem and not its
production cost; and the corpus gives every tenant the same size, so the join's cost does
not grow with a neighbour's data the way it would in a real account distribution.

Its paired count `Q4` is a **different** shape, and the distinction matters because it is
what the after phase gets compared against. `Q4` does not scan `Post` sequentially at all:

```text
Aggregate
  Nested Loop
    Seq Scan          rel=Project  rows=100  loops=1
    Index Only Scan   rel=Post  idx=Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx  rows=95  loops=100
```

It walks `Project` and probes `Post` — the inverse of a `Post` scan. At 1.279 ms its cost is
the 100-loop fan-out over a sequentially scanned `Project`, so it belongs with `Q6` below
rather than with `Q3`.

### Every other `Post` read is already served on the `Post` side

| Case | Path                          | Index chosen on `Post`          | Exec (ms) |
| ---- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------- | --------- |
| Q1   | `listByProject` page          | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx`  | 0.036     |
| Q2   | `listByProject` count         | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` | 0.035     |
| Q4   | `listGlobal` count            | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` | 1.279     |
| Q5   | `findByProjectId` page        | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx`  | 0.021     |
| Q7   | `findOwnerAccountId`          | `Post_pkey` + `Project_pkey`    | 0.016     |
| Q8   | `getProjectStats` (DRAFT arm) | `Post_projectId_status_idx`     | 0.012     |

`Q4` sits in this table because its `Post` access IS indexed — an Index Only Scan on
`Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx`. What makes it three orders of magnitude slower than its
neighbours is the OTHER side of its join, which is the next finding.

### `Q4` and `Q6` both pay a sequential scan on `Project`

Two captures reach a `Seq Scan` on `Project`, and neither is the feed:

| Case | Path                 | Shape                                                    | Exec (ms) |
| ---- | -------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | --------- |
| Q4   | `listGlobal` count   | Nested Loop: `Seq Scan Project` → 100× Index Only `Post` | 1.279     |
| Q6   | `filterIdsByAccount` | Hash Join: Index Scan `Post_pkey` × `Seq Scan Project`   | 0.151     |

On this corpus that is 517 project rows and cheap in absolute terms, but the shape is the
point. `Q6` is the **bulk-mutation tenant gate** — the read that decides which ids a caller
is allowed to touch — and `Q4` is the account-wide count. Neither can answer its question
without walking into another table, because the tenant identity is not on `Post`.

That is the argument for the composite key stated at its strongest: it does not merely make
the feed faster, it removes the `Project` walk from the **tenant gate itself**. `Q3` needs
the new partial index; `Q4` and `Q6` need the `accountId` column to exist on `Post` at all.

### The baseline had to be made reproducible first, and that is a finding

The first version of this capture ran `ANALYZE` after seeding. Re-running it over the
**identical, untouched corpus** produced two different plans: `Q2` moved from a Bitmap Heap
Scan to an Index Only Scan (0.081 → 0.030 ms) and `Q4` from a hash join over two sequential
scans to a nested loop with an Index Only Scan (3.05 → 1.32 ms). Nothing about the data
changed. The freshly bulk-inserted heap pages were not yet marked all-visible, so index-only
paths were unavailable until autovacuum reached them.

A baseline that changes shape on its own is not a baseline: the after phase would have read
that drift as an effect of the migration. The seeder now runs `VACUUM (ANALYZE)`, which sets
the visibility map before the capture. Verified rather than assumed, and re-verified since:
every independent full-cleanup-and-reseed capture taken against **this pre-migration schema**
reproduced its 13 plans with **identical node sequences and index choices**. The sharpest check
came when `Q13` was repointed at a parent that actually has media — the other **12 cases came
back identical down to `Actual Rows` and `Actual Loops`**, with only `Q13` moving, and only
from `rows=0` to `rows=1` on the same node and the same index.

That scoping to the pre-migration schema is deliberate and is not a hedge. `VACUUM` removes
the visibility-map drift; it does not make every plan stable, and the post-migration schema
introduced one case where it is not — `Q4` lands on a 0.11 % cost tie there and flips between
captures. The after reading states that under §"Reproducibility, and the noise band this
capture can support". The claim here is about these plans, not about every plan this harness
can produce. Execution times drift inside run-to-run noise
(single-digit microseconds on the sub-millisecond cases), which is why the tables in this
section quote the median of the capture's three runs and why the SHAPE, not the number, is
what the after phase must be compared on. Anyone re-running this should use the documented
command, which includes the seeding step, rather than capturing against a corpus someone else
left warm.

### The policy qual is present, which is what makes these plans the application's plans

Every plan that touches `Project` carries the row-security predicate verbatim in its
`Filter`, for example on `Q3`:

```text
(("accountId" = 'tif-ab-a'::text)
 AND ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text)
      OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true))))
```

That is the proof the capture really ran as `omnipost_app` with the GUC bound. A capture
taken as the owner would show the left half only, and every comparison against the after
phase would be measuring the wrong thing.

### `Post`, `PostContent` and `PostMedia` carry no policy at all today

The data-shape table records `relrowsecurity = false` and `0` policies for all three. That
is the pre-migration state, and it is the reason the child-read plans below are as clean as
they are — there is no qual to apply. It is also the single largest source of change in the
after phase: the trio becomes RLS-covered, so every plan in this file gains a qual it does
not have here.

### The child reads all index-scan today — which is the exemption's starting point

`Q9`-`Q13` are the evidence the leg-1 shape-1b index exemption's revisit trigger governs.
Today every one of them is a plain `Index Scan` (or `Index Only Scan` for the aggregate) on
the parent key, between 0.011 ms and 0.090 ms (median of three runs):

| Case | Read                                 | Index                                    | Rows | Exec (ms) |
| ---- | ------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- | ---- | --------- |
| Q9   | `PostContent` by 20 parent ids       | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` | 20   | 0.090     |
| Q10  | `PostContent` by one parent id       | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` | 1    | 0.011     |
| Q11  | `PostMedia` by 20 parent ids         | `PostMedia_postId_idx`                   | 6    | 0.076     |
| Q12  | `PostMedia` `_count` over 20 parents | `PostMedia_postId_idx` (Index Only Scan) | 6    | 0.085     |
| Q13  | `PostMedia` by one parent id         | `PostMedia_postId_idx`                   | 1    | 0.011     |

The `Rows` column is in the table because it was the defect. `Q13` originally pointed at the
page's newest post, and media is seeded only for every third post — so it matched **nothing**
on both sides. Its digest compared an empty string to an empty string, which is a comparison
no mirror error can turn red, over a plan whose `Actual Rows` was `0`. A lookup that finds
nothing cannot demonstrate degradation, so as captured it was not evidence for the revisit
trigger at all. It now runs against a parent that actually carries media, and the harness
refuses to write a report containing a case that matched nothing unless the case declares
that emptiness deliberate (see §Method). `Q11` and `Q12` were always non-empty, so the
`PostMedia` side of the trigger has three live rows rather than two.

Note that `PostContent` is served by the `(postId, locale, revision)` UNIQUE index rather
than the `(postId, locale)` index — the planner's choice, recorded because a change in it
after the migration would otherwise look like a regression.

The trigger the design wrote is specific: **if the after-phase plans show the RLS policy
qual degrading either table to a sequential scan, the accountId-led index is added in the
same slice.** These five rows are what "degrading" is measured from. The expected benign
outcome is the same `Index Scan` node with the policy predicate appearing as an extra
`Filter` on it; a `Seq Scan` or a `Bitmap Heap Scan` replacing it is the trigger firing.

### One thing this capture deliberately does not settle

Whether Prisma issues those child reads as separate `postId IN (...)` statements or folds
them into the parent query as lateral joins was not measured. The `relationJoins` preview
feature is enabled in `infra/prisma/schema.prisma`, so both are possible, and this harness
cannot see Prisma's emitted SQL (see §Method). The cases above measure the child read as its
own statement, which is the shape the exemption's argument is written about; if the after
phase needs the other shape, the way to get it is a capture with
`relationLoadStrategy` stated explicitly on both sides, not an inference from these numbers.

## After — post-migration capture

Captured 2026-09-09T06:39:18.240Z in 0.2 s. PostgreSQL: PostgreSQL 16.14 (Debian 16.14-1.pgdg12+1). Measuring role: `omnipost_app` (non-superuser, non-bypassing), `app.account_id` bound to `tif-ab-a` for every statement below.

**Re-run this exact capture:**

```bash
pnpm db:up
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --phase after --projects 100 --posts 10000 --runs 3
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --cleanup
# The generator emits unpadded Markdown tables and Prettier aligns them, so the
# capture ALWAYS leaves this file formatting-dirty. Run this or the repo gate fails:
pnpm exec prettier --write docs/reports/TENANT_RLS_AB_MEASUREMENT.md
```

### Data shape

Two tenants (`tif-ab-a`, `tif-ab-b`), 100 projects and 10000 posts each, posts round-robined over the tenant's projects; every 20th post soft-deleted, every 10th archived, statuses cycling DRAFT/SCHEDULED/PUBLISHED/FAILED, one `PostContent` per post and one `PostMedia` per third post. `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` ran on all five tables before the capture, so the visibility map is set and index-only-scan costing reflects a settled heap. That is ALL it buys, and the previous wording claimed more: it said the plan was "reproducible across reseeds", which this artifact's own data disproves — `Q4` selects a different index between runs of ONE capture, on a 0.11 % planner tie that nothing here repairs. Where that happens the case carries a per-run index annotation.

| Namespaced rows                               | Count |
| --------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| `Account` (`tif-ab-%`)                        | 2     |
| `Project` (`tif-ab-%`)                        | 200   |
| `Post` (`tif-ab-%`)                           | 20000 |
| `PostContent` (`tif-ab-%`)                    | 20000 |
| `PostMedia` (`tif-ab-%`)                      | 6666  |
| _control: all `Account` rows in the database_ | 356   |

| Table         | Live rows | Planner estimate | Total size | RLS  | FORCE | Owner      | Policies |
| ------------- | --------- | ---------------- | ---------- | ---- | ----- | ---------- | -------- |
| `Project`     | 517       | 517              | 31 MB      | true | false | `postgres` | 1        |
| `Post`        | 20003     | 20003            | 20 MB      | true | false | `postgres` | 1        |
| `PostContent` | 20003     | 20003            | 24 MB      | true | false | `postgres` | 1        |
| `PostMedia`   | 6666      | 6666             | 3888 kB    | true | false | `postgres` | 1        |

### Hot `Post` listing paths

#### Q1 — listByProject — page 1 of a project's live posts

- **Source site**: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaPostQueryRepository.ts:152 (where built at :186)`
- **Why it is here**: the customer's post list; the most frequently served Post read. EMISSION CHANGED with the trio's tenant column: `buildWhereClause` filtered the RELATION (`project: { accountId }`) when §Before was captured and filters the LOCAL `Post.accountId` now, so this case's query text differs between the two phases
- **Mirror fidelity**: match — the case's own comparison returned `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the Prisma call and `tif-ab-a-post-000001,tif-ab-a-post-000101,tif-ab-a-post-000201,tif-ab-a-post-000301,tif-ab-a-post-000401,tif-ab-a-post-0… (419 chars)` from the mirror.
- **Prisma call wall time**: 24.85 ms
- **Planning time (ms, per run)**: 0.070 / 0.063 / 0.065
- **Execution time (ms, per run)**: 0.100 / 0.086 / 0.084
- **Plan nodes**: Limit → Sort → Index Scan
- **Indexes used**: Post_accountId_projectId_idx

Query text (the mirror; `EXPLAIN` is taken from exactly this):

```sql
SELECT p.id, p."projectId", p.status, p."scheduledAt", p."publishedAt", p."createdAt", p."updatedAt" FROM "Post" p
 WHERE p."projectId" = 'tif-ab-a-proj-0001'
   AND p."accountId" = 'tif-ab-a'
   AND p."deletedAt" IS NULL
   AND p."archivedAt" IS NULL
 ORDER BY p."createdAt" DESC
 LIMIT 20 OFFSET 0
```

SELECT p.id, p."projectId", p.status, p."scheduledAt", p."publishedAt", p."createdAt", p."updatedAt" FROM "Post" p

### `postId`-led child reads — `PostContent` and `PostMedia`

These two tables are the ones the leg-1 index exemption governs: the design gives them no accountId-led index, on the argument that their reads are parent-key-led and equality is LEAKPROOF, so the caller's filter still index-scans ahead of the policy qual. The plans below are the evidence that argument gets compared against once the columns and policies land.

## Reading the after capture

Hand-written, outside the generated block, so a re-run of `--phase after` does not overwrite
it. Every number below is a MEDIAN of the capture's three runs, read from the plan TREES in
the block above rather than from their flattened one-line summaries — a node sequence
rendered as `A → B → C` loses which side of a join each scan sits on, and the before section
records a case where reading the summary instead of the tree inverted a join twice.

### Two things changed, not one — and which cases that affects

The migration is not the only difference between the two blocks. PR 5 also moved four
repository call sites onto the tenant column it had just added, so for those four cases the
before/after delta is a delta of TWO changes at once. The mirror follows the source site as
it is today (a mirror kept at the old text would take a plan for a query the application no
longer issues, and the fidelity check could not notice, because both texts return the SAME
rows and the check compares row identity), so this is stated rather than avoided:

| Case        | Emission when §Before was captured              | Emission now          | Why it moved                                                                                                       |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Q1`, `Q2`  | `project: { accountId }` — a relation predicate | `accountId` on `Post` | `buildWhereClause` filters the local column                                                                        |
| `Q5`, `Q8`  | no account predicate at all                     | `accountId` on `Post` | the required `TenantScope` puts the tenant in the `where`                                                          |
| the other 9 | unchanged                                       | unchanged             | `listGlobal`, `filterIdsByAccount`, `findOwnerAccountId` and every child read still emit exactly what they emitted |

So `Q3`, `Q4`, `Q6`, `Q7` and `Q9`–`Q13` carry a delta attributable to the SCHEMA alone —
the column, the indexes, and the policy. `Q1`, `Q2`, `Q5` and `Q8` do not, and no conclusion
below rests on them alone.

### Reproducibility, and the noise band this capture can support

The after phase was captured TWICE for this report, each time from a full cleanup and reseed,
exactly as the before phase was; the block above is the second. An independent re-gate then
captured it **twice more** under the same command. Across all **four** independent captures:

**12 of the 13 cases are stable — and `Q4` is not.** The 12 reproduce with identical plan node
sequences and identical index choices, medians moving at most **6 µs** on the sub-millisecond
cases and under **1 %** on `Q3`. Nothing smaller than that is claimed as a change below, which
is why the child reads are reported as flat even where their third decimal moved.

`Q4` is a **coin-flip**, and the reason is measured rather than inferred. Its two candidate
paths are separated by **0.11 %**:

```text
Seq Scan on "Post"                             (cost=0.00..708.06)
Index Only Scan  Post_accountId_projectId_idx  (cost=0.29..707.29)   <- forced, enable_seqscan=off
```

Costed on the live corpus as `omnipost_app` with the GUC bound. **0.77 cost units out of 708**
separate the two, and the planner's preference between them is the entire content of `Q4`'s
"index choice".

A one-page `relpages` drift is enough to flip which one wins, and that drift is real between
reseeds rather than hypothetical: the same `Project` sequential scan was estimated at 23.63
rows in one capture and 21.63 in another. The two captures for this report both chose the
index-only path (the `Q4` plan in the generated block above, and quoted again under §"The fact
that governs every reading below"); the two re-gate captures both chose the sequential
scan, reporting `Indexes used: (none)` and 5.42 / 5.40 ms. **Neither is the wrong answer** —
the planner is picking between two paths it costs as equal, and the harness does not control
the input that decides it.

This was foreseeable from the harness's own method notes, which is the more useful half of the
finding: the `vacuumAnalyze` docblock (`scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts:377-384`) already names
`Q4` as one of the two plans that moved on their own during baseline work. The fix it
documents — `VACUUM` over bare `ANALYZE` — removed the visibility-map drift, and this is a
different mechanism (a genuine cost tie) that it neither addressed nor claimed to. **`Q4`'s
plan choice is therefore an illustration everywhere it appears below, never a proof**, and
every conclusion that once leaned on it is re-based on the 12 stable cases.

### The 13-case comparison

| Case   | Plan-shape delta                                                                        | Index on the measured table                                                         | Exec median (ms)  | Δ             | Emission  |
| ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------- | ------------- | --------- |
| `Q1`   | Nested Loop + Materialize into `Project` **gone**; a top-N `Sort` **appears**           | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx` → **`Post_accountId_projectId_idx`**                 | 0.036 → 0.086     | **×2.39**     | changed   |
| `Q2`   | Nested Loop into `Project` **gone**; Index Only Scan → Index Scan                       | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` → **`Post_accountId_projectId_idx`**                | 0.035 → 0.058     | **×1.66**     | changed   |
| `Q3`   | **identical node sequence** — still `Seq Scan` on `Post` hash-joined to `Project`       | none → none                                                                         | 4.305 → 6.754     | **×1.57**     | unchanged |
| `Q4` † | Nested Loop → Hash Join; `Post` side becomes a FULL index-only scan **or a `Seq Scan`** | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` → **`Post_accountId_projectId_idx`** _or none_      | 1.279 → 4.77–5.42 | **×3.7–×4.2** | unchanged |
| `Q5`   | a top-N `Sort` **appears**                                                              | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx` → **`Post_accountId_projectId_idx`**                 | 0.021 → 0.074     | **×3.52**     | changed   |
| `Q6`   | identical node sequence                                                                 | `Post_pkey` → `Post_pkey`                                                           | 0.151 → 0.154     | ×1.02         | unchanged |
| `Q7`   | identical shape; the `Project` probe becomes an **Index Only Scan**                     | `Post_pkey` + `Project_pkey` → `Post_id_accountId_key` + `Project_id_accountId_key` | 0.016 → 0.020     | ×1.25         | unchanged |
| `Q8`   | Index Only Scan → Index Scan                                                            | `Post_projectId_status_idx` (unchanged)                                             | 0.012 → 0.014     | ×1.17         | changed   |
| `Q9`   | identical — `Index Scan`, policy predicate added as a `Filter`                          | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` (unchanged)                                | 0.090 → 0.100     | ×1.11         | unchanged |
| `Q10`  | identical — `Index Scan`, policy predicate added as a `Filter`                          | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` (unchanged)                                | 0.011 → 0.012     | ×1.09         | unchanged |
| `Q11`  | identical — `Index Scan`, policy predicate added as a `Filter`                          | `PostMedia_postId_idx` (unchanged)                                                  | 0.076 → 0.075     | ×0.99         | unchanged |
| `Q12`  | Index Only Scan → Index Scan; index and index condition unchanged                       | `PostMedia_postId_idx` (unchanged)                                                  | 0.085 → 0.086     | ×1.01         | unchanged |
| `Q13`  | identical — `Index Scan`, policy predicate added as a `Filter`                          | `PostMedia_postId_idx` (unchanged)                                                  | 0.011 → 0.012     | ×1.09         | unchanged |

† **`Q4`'s row is the unstable one** (see §Reproducibility above). Its index CHOICE flips
between reseeds on a 0.11 % cost tie; what does **not** flip is the regression — `Q4` costs
between ×3.7 and ×4.2 its pre-migration 1.279 ms whichever path the planner takes, because
both paths now evaluate the policy qual per row. Read the row for its Δ, not for its index
name.

`Q1` and `Q5` are the only two cases whose Δ is above the noise band AND whose emission also
changed. Both are explained by the index choice rather than by the new predicate, which the
next section shows.

### The fact that governs every reading below: the policy qual is never an index condition

In all 13 after-plans the row-security predicate appears as a **`Filter`**, never as an
`Index Cond`. That is structural, not incidental: the shipped policy is a disjunction whose
first arm (`current_setting('app.account_id', true) = '__system__'`) mentions no column, and
PostgreSQL cannot turn a disjunction with a non-indexable arm into an index condition. Counted
over every node of every plan in the block above, and independently recounted by the re-gate:
the qual lands **17× as a `Filter` and 0× as an `Index Cond`**.

**`Q1`, `Q2` and `Q5` are the proof**, and they are chosen deliberately over the more dramatic
`Q4` because all three reproduce identically across every capture. Each of them carries an
`accountId` index condition AND the policy qual as a filter, on the same node. From `Q1`'s
plan above:

```text
Index Scan  rel=Post  idx=Post_accountId_projectId_idx
  Index Cond: (("accountId" = 'tif-ab-a') AND ("projectId" = 'tif-ab-a-proj-0001'))
  Filter:     (("archivedAt" IS NULL)
               AND ((current_setting('app.account_id', true) = '__system__')
                    OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id', true))))
```

The **same column** is on both lines, which is what makes this decisive. `accountId` reaches
the index condition because the QUERY supplies it as a literal; row security's own reference to
that identical column stays in the filter and is evaluated per row anyway. So even in the best
case available — the tenant column indexed, and the index chosen — the policy is not served by
the index. No planner coin-flip can change that, because both halves are in the same plan node.

`Q4` **illustrates** what that fact costs, and nothing more: its plan choice is the unstable
one (§Reproducibility). In the captures where the planner picks `Post_accountId_projectId_idx`:

```text
Aggregate
  Hash Join
    Index Only Scan  rel=Post  idx=Post_accountId_projectId_idx
                     Filter = the policy qual
                     Actual Rows 9500   Rows Removed by Filter 9500
    Hash
      Seq Scan       rel=Project  rows=100  Rows Removed by Filter 417
```

it reads **19 000 index entries to keep 9 500** — no tenant selectivity, scanned end to end and
then filtered. In the captures where it picks a sequential scan the same 9 500 rows are filtered
away off the heap instead. Vivid either way, load-bearing neither way: every other conclusion in
this section rests on `Q1`/`Q2`/`Q5`.

### Verdict (a) — `Q3` did NOT move onto the new index

**No, and on this corpus it could not have.** `Q3` (`listGlobal` page 1) has the same node
sequence it had before the migration — `Limit → Sort → Hash Join → Seq Scan (Post) → Hash →
Seq Scan (Project)` — and reports `Indexes used: (none)`. Its execution median went from
4.305 ms to 6.754 ms, and the whole increase is on the `Post` sequential scan, which now
evaluates the policy qual on every one of the 20 003 rows it reads (`Rows Removed by Filter:
10503`, up from a `deletedAt`-only filter before).

Two independent reasons, and BOTH have to be fixed for the index to serve this query:

1. **The query still asks the old question.** `listGlobal` was deliberately left unconverted
   (its tenant predicate remains `project: { accountId, deletedAt: null }`), so there is no
   `accountId` predicate on `Post` for an `(accountId, projectId)` index to answer. The
   liveness half of that predicate is real and cannot simply be dropped: a soft-deleted
   project's posts keep `deletedAt IS NULL` themselves, so the join is doing work the local
   column does not replace.
2. **The policy cannot supply the missing predicate.** Per the fact above, the policy qual is
   a `Filter`. Even a `listGlobal` that supplied `accountId` locally would get the index
   condition from the QUERY, never from row security. That is not an inference: `Q1`, `Q2` and
   `Q5` are three queries that DO supply it locally, and every one of them still carries the
   qual as a per-row filter on the same node the index condition sits on.

This is the design's own "regression appears with its adjudication" clause, and the
adjudication is recorded in the last section of this reading rather than left implicit.

### Verdict (b) — no child read regressed to a sequential scan; the trigger is checked and did NOT fire

The leg-1 shape-1b index exemption's revisit trigger is: _if the after-phase plans show the
RLS policy qual degrading either table to a sequential scan, the accountId-led index is added
in the same slice._ Measured against the five baselines the before section recorded:

| Case  | Node before     | Node after | Index                                    | Policy qual lands as | Rows Removed by Filter | Exec (ms)     |
| ----- | --------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------- | ------------- |
| `Q9`  | Index Scan      | Index Scan | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` | `Filter`             | 0                      | 0.090 → 0.100 |
| `Q10` | Index Scan      | Index Scan | `PostContent_postId_locale_revision_key` | `Filter`             | 0                      | 0.011 → 0.012 |
| `Q11` | Index Scan      | Index Scan | `PostMedia_postId_idx`                   | `Filter`             | 0                      | 0.076 → 0.075 |
| `Q12` | Index Only Scan | Index Scan | `PostMedia_postId_idx`                   | `Filter`             | 0                      | 0.085 → 0.086 |
| `Q13` | Index Scan      | Index Scan | `PostMedia_postId_idx`                   | `Filter`             | 0                      | 0.011 → 0.012 |

**No `Seq Scan`. No `Bitmap Heap Scan`. The trigger did not fire**, and this is the outcome
the before section named in advance as the benign one: the same index scan, with the policy
predicate appearing as an extra `Filter` on it. `Rows Removed by Filter: 0` on all five says
the qual discards nothing, because the parent-key equality has already restricted the read to
rows of the caller's own tenant — which is exactly the argument the exemption was granted on,
now measured instead of asserted.

**One node type did change, and it is recorded rather than absorbed.** `Q12` moved from
`Index Only Scan` to `Index Scan`. The index condition, the index, and the row count are all
identical; what changed is that the policy qual reads `accountId`, which
`PostMedia_postId_idx` does not carry, so the heap has to be visited to evaluate it. The
same thing happens to `Q8` on `Post` (`Post_projectId_status_idx` carries no `accountId`
either). Both cost about 1-2 µs on this corpus — inside the noise band — and neither is a
sequential scan, so neither is the trigger. It is written down because it is the shape a
future regression would take first: an index-only path lost to a qual the index cannot cover.

The exemption therefore **stands**, and its record in
`docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` is updated with these five rows as the evidence that
justifies it.

### Verdict (c) — the design's cost claim for the ONE new index is NOT borne out

D-S1-1 justified `@@index([accountId, projectId], where: { deletedAt: null })` with: _"It
serves the RLS policy's tenant qual AND tenant-wide listings."_ Both halves were measured,
and both fail on this corpus:

| The claim                             | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| "serves the RLS policy's tenant qual" | **No.** The qual is a `Filter` in all 13 plans (17× `Filter`, 0× `Index Cond`). `Q1`/`Q2`/`Q5` settle it: they put `accountId` into the index condition from the query, and the policy still filters per row on that same node. `Q4` illustrates the cost — 9 500 of 19 000 entries filtered away — but its plan choice flips between reseeds, so it is not carrying this row. |
| "AND tenant-wide listings"            | **No, as shipped.** The tenant-wide listing (`Q3`) and its count (`Q4`) reach the tenant through the relation, so the index answers neither question.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

What the index actually does on this corpus is displace better-fitting indexes on the four
`projectId`-led paths that now supply `accountId` as a literal:

| Case   | It replaced                     | What was lost                                                                                                                                                            | Cost              |
| ------ | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------- |
| `Q1`   | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx`  | the `createdAt` ordering, so `LIMIT 20` no longer stops the scan early — 100 rows are read and top-N sorted instead of 20                                                | 0.036 → 0.086     |
| `Q5`   | `Post_projectId_createdAt_idx`  | the same ordering, same consequence                                                                                                                                      | 0.021 → 0.074     |
| `Q2`   | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` | index-only coverage of `archivedAt`                                                                                                                                      | 0.035 → 0.058     |
| `Q4` † | `Post_projectId_archivedAt_idx` | the nested loop's early exit — when the new index is chosen it is scanned end to end; when it is not, the scan is sequential and the same rows are filtered off the heap | 1.279 → 4.77–5.42 |

**Net, in one sentence: the one new index is chosen by three to four of the eight `Post`
listing cases depending on the reseed, is slower in every case that chooses it, is worth
0.1 % where it is chosen most narrowly, and is not used by the one case it was designed for.**

Each clause is a measurement. **Three to four**: `Q1`, `Q2` and `Q5` choose it in every
capture; `Q4` † chooses it in some and not others. **Slower in every case that chooses it**:
×2.39, ×1.66, ×3.52 and (when it takes it) ×3.73 — there is no capture in which choosing it
made a case faster. **Worth 0.1 %**: at `Q4`'s tie the planner costs the index-only path at
707.29 against the sequential scan's 708.06, so on the one case where the choice is close
enough to flip, the index is buying **0.77 cost units out of 708**. That is the honest ceiling
on what this index does for these queries, and it is a stronger statement than the count.

Three caveats that keep this honest rather than louder than the evidence. Every absolute
number here is sub-millisecond except `Q3`/`Q4`, on a 20 000-row table, so these are SHAPES
and not production costs. The corpus gives every project exactly 100 posts, which is the
condition under which `(projectId, createdAt)` beats `(accountId, projectId)`; a project with
far more posts, or an account with far fewer projects, moves that comparison. And the index's
justification was never only read performance — it is also the shape a future
`accountId`-led sweep and the hard-delete cascade would use.

### The regressions, and the adjudication of each

The artifact does not get to contain only the favourable comparisons, so here is the whole
list, each with a named disposition. **None of the three repairs below could be authored in
this work unit**: two are migrations and one is a production query change with its own tests,
and this unit is `docs/` plus the evidence harness. They are recorded as owned follow-ups
with the measurement that motivates each, which is the difference between an adjudication and
a silence.

| Regression                                                               | Cause                                                                                   | Adjudication                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Q3` +2.449 ms, `Q4` +3.49 to +4.14 ms †                                 | the policy qual is evaluated per row and is not indexable                               | **Rewrite the policy's GUC reads as scalar subqueries.** Measured, not proposed: the form decision run below runs that exact form (arm `W`), and on the same sequential scan node it is **~2.1-2.4× cheaper** — 1.833 ms and 1.775 ms across two independent captures, against 3.881–4.146 ms for the shipped form. With the scan-node median now computed, the decision run puts the same comparison at **2.31× on the scan node** (4.163 → 1.804 ms, pooled over 15 samples) and **1.82× on statement time** (5.438 → 2.993 ms) — the band is drawn from the SCAN-NODE statistic, so a statement-time reading outside it is the statistic differing, not the policy regressing. It is a migration, so it is a follow-up, not this unit. |
| `Q3` does not use the new index                                          | `listGlobal` still expresses its tenant through the relation                            | **Reshape the query** to filter the local `accountId` while keeping the project-liveness predicate. Production code with its own tests; a follow-up.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Q1` ×2.39, `Q5` ×3.52, `Q2` ×1.66, `Q4` ×3.73 † when it takes the index | the new index displaces `projectId`-led indexes that also supplied ordering or coverage | **Index shape decision**, which is a schema change and therefore token-gated. The two candidates the evidence supports are extending the index to `(accountId, projectId, createdAt)` or dropping it until a query actually needs it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Q8`, `Q12` lose their index-only path                                   | the qual reads `accountId`, absent from both indexes                                    | **Accepted.** 1-2 µs on this corpus, inside the noise band, and the alternative is widening two indexes to carry a column their queries do not filter on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

The first three rows have a durable home, so they are not findings that live only inside a
change record: **SMELL-93** in `docs/reports/roadmap-detected-smells-backlog.md` carries the
measurements above, the four-arm proof that identifies the per-row `current_setting()` call
as the expense, and the three repairs sequenced smallest-first. It is filed as an
OPTIMIZATION rather than a defect: every plan in this report returns the correct rows, and
the isolation this slice exists to deliver is not in question anywhere in it.

## Policy form decision run — `A′` vs `W` vs `S`

Captured 2026-09-10T01:58:27.936Z in 1.2 s. PostgreSQL: PostgreSQL 16.14 (Debian 16.14-1.pgdg12+1). Every arm installs its policy on all three trio tables (`Post`, `PostContent`, `PostMedia`) inside ONE transaction, reaches `omnipost_app` with `SET LOCAL ROLE` in that same transaction, binds `app.account_id` to `tif-ab-a`, measures, and ROLLS BACK. Nothing is committed; the restore is the rollback itself, and the shipped policies are re-read afterwards and compared.

Each arm measures **16 probes**: the three synthetic shapes below, which carry no tenant predicate of their own so the policy is the whole restriction, and the **13 application cases** of the capture sections — entered as their SQL mirrors, because a Prisma call cannot run inside the arm's owner transaction. The mirrors' fidelity to their repository calls is proven on every capture run, and this run consumes that proof rather than restating it.

The arm swaps ALL THREE trio tables. An earlier form swapped `Post` alone, which was sound while only the three shapes ran; five of the 13 cases read `PostContent` or `PostMedia`, and measuring those against a bare child policy under every arm would put five rows in this report whose numbers cannot respond to the arm at all.

**Re-run this exact comparison:**

```bash
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --policy-ab --projects 100 --posts 10000 --runs 3 --repetitions 5
node --import tsx --conditions development --env-file=.env \
  scripts/rls-ab-measurement.ts --cleanup
pnpm exec prettier --write docs/reports/TENANT_RLS_AB_MEASUREMENT.md
```

### The arms

| Arm  | Form                                                                  | `USING (...)`                                                                                                                       | Note                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `A′` | shipped local-column policy                                           | _(the shipped policy, untouched)_                                                                                                   | the policy `20260909000500_add_rls_post_trio` installed, measured WITHOUT being re-created — the control is the live object, not a copy of it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `W`  | both halves wrapped — the disjunction, each GUC read hoisted          | `(SELECT current_setting('app.account_id', true)) = '__system__' OR "accountId" = (SELECT current_setting('app.account_id', true))` | SEMANTICALLY IDENTICAL to the shipped policy — same column, same `__system__` escape — differing only in that each `current_setting()` is wrapped in `(SELECT ...)`, which PostgreSQL evaluates once as an InitPlan instead of once per candidate row. This arm was measured as `A′+init` in the retired four-arm comparison; the numbers under the old name are the same form                                              |
| `S`  | single hoisted read, set-membership against the two admissible values | `(SELECT current_setting('app.account_id', true)) IN ('__system__', "accountId")`                                                   | the same predicate written so the GUC is read ONCE syntactically: a row is visible when the bound value is either the `__system__` sentinel or the row's own account. Text equality is symmetric, so this is `x = '__system__' OR x = "accountId"`, which is the shipped `"accountId" = x` order. It is here to answer whether one wrapped read plans better than two — a question the design refused to settle by argument |

### Restore proof

The three `tenant_isolation` policies, read from `pg_policies` before the first arm and again after the last one. The comparison is over the FIVE attributes that define a policy — `(qual, with_check, permissive, cmd, roles)` — because an arm re-creates with `USING (...)` alone, which leaves `qual` byte-identical while dropping `with_check` to `null`; a `qual`-only proof passes on exactly that swap:

```text
Post:
  qual       = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  with_check = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  permissive = PERMISSIVE
  cmd        = ALL
  roles      = {public}
PostContent:
  qual       = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  with_check = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  permissive = PERMISSIVE
  cmd        = ALL
  roles      = {public}
PostMedia:
  qual       = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  with_check = ((current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true) = '__system__'::text) OR ("accountId" = current_setting('app.account_id'::text, true)))
  permissive = PERMISSIVE
  cmd        = ALL
  roles      = {public}
```

The two reads are identical, which is what makes the swap provably transaction-scoped.

### The form verdict

**Computed from the medians above, not typed in.** The rule is applied in code (`decideForm`), so this section cannot say one thing while the table says another.

- **The band**: a move is INSIDE it when it is ≤ 6 µs **or** < 1 % of its own baseline. The two limits are an OR: an absolute-only band calls every sub-millisecond probe out-of-band for a few microseconds of wobble, and a relative-only band calls a 7 µs probe a 100 % regression.
- **The statistic**: the SCAN-NODE median over the POOLED 15 sample(s) — 3 EXPLAIN run(s) × 5 independent sweep(s) — per probe, on that probe's own measured relation. Statement medians are in the tables above and are NOT interchangeable with these — the ratio between the two differs by case, which is why a band drawn on one cannot be read against the other.
- **The direction**: `W` is the baseline and `S` is the candidate, because the pre-declared tiebreak is stated in that direction.
- **Attributability**: a difference counts as the FORM's only when it is out of band AND every sweep agreed on its sign. This is not a precaution, it is a measured necessity: six consecutive sweeps of this comparison put `Q4` anywhere from 111 µs in `S`'s favour to 112 µs in `W`'s, so a rule reading one sweep announced `S`, `W` or `UNDECIDED` depending only on which sweep it read.
- **The tiebreak**: pre-declared in task 2.7 BEFORE this run — when no difference is attributable, `W` wins, because it is the textually minimal delta from the shipped form, it keeps the 58-policy sweep a pure "wrap each `current_setting` call" transform identical for the standard and the `AIPromptTemplate` variant, and it keeps the form-uniformity gate matcher one adjacency rule.

#### InitPlan count per arm — read from the plan, not assumed

`design.md` refused to assume whether `W`'s two wrapped reads share one InitPlan and asked the plan to answer. It answers here, per arm, from the `Subplan Name` labels the walk collects. The labels are kept PER RUN, so a run-to-run disagreement is annotated rather than resolved to whichever run was last.

| ---- | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |

| `W`  | 2                               | InitPlan 1 (returns $0), InitPlan 2 (returns $1) |
| `S`  | 2                               | InitPlan 1 (returns $0), InitPlan 2 (returns $1) |

#### Semantic equivalence of the two forms

The 13-case comparison runs with `app.account_id` bound to ONE tenant, and under a bound tenant an `S` body that had lost its `__system__` member returns exactly the same rows — every digest matches and a form that revoked every `withSystemContext()` flow's visibility would ship with a green run behind it. These three states are the ones the case run cannot reach. The expressions are read from the arm list, so this proves the bodies the arms install; the sample is a literal 3-row set (local tenant, foreign tenant, NULL account) rather than `Post`, so the comparison cannot be filtered by the policy under test and can include the NULL-account row that the `AIPromptTemplate` variant depends on.

| GUC state                      | Admitted by `W`                       | Admitted by `S`                       | Three-valued divergences |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| bound tenant `tif-ab-a`        | `r1-local`                            | `r1-local`                            | 0                        |
| `__system__` sentinel          | `r1-local,r2-foreign,r3-null-account` | `r1-local,r2-foreign,r3-null-account` | 0                        |
| UNSET (no `set_config` at all) | `(none)`                              | `(none)`                              | 0                        |

#### Verdict

**`W` wins.** no probe's difference is attributable to the form — 1 probe(s) moved outside the band but their sign FLIPPED between repetitions (S3), which measures the run rather than the form, so the two forms are indistinguishable on this corpus and the PRE-DECLARED tiebreak decides. The winning body, read from the arm list rather than retyped beside it: `USING ((SELECT current_setting('app.account_id', true)) = '__system__' OR "accountId" = (SELECT current_setting('app.account_id', true)))` — so the verdict names the exact predicate the trio migration installs, without a lookup.

**1 out-of-band probe(s)** on the pooled medians, each owed a named adjudication:

- `S3` — 20.0 µs (1.11 %), sign FLIPPED between sweeps, so it measures the run and not the form: **accepted as noise**, and it is the reason the tiebreak governs rather than this number.

`Q8` is a declared miss probe: it counts zero on this corpus under every arm, and the run refuses to write if it ever starts matching rows.

### The three synthetic shapes, per arm

- **Rows returned (identical under every arm)**: 1

 WHERE p.id = '<the page''s newest post>'
